<<<SUMMARY>>>
Valero Energy (VLO), Par Pacific Holdings (PARR), and PBF Energy (PBF) are capitalizing on elevated refining margins, low product inventories, and resilient transportation fuel demand. VLO's Gulf Coast refineries benefit from processing discounted heavy-sour crude, while its complex refining network provides flexibility. <<<INSIGHTS>>>

However, sentiment is tempered by risks from rising crude and freight costs.
PARR | positive | PARR operates an integrated downstream energy business with commercial flexibility across its refining and retail operations, allowing it to capitalize on elevated margin environments and changing market conditions.
PBF | positive | PBF operates a geographically diverse refining network with large-scale capacity and complex refining systems across six refineries, providing exposure to multiple regional markets and supporting higher margins in the current elevated margin environment.

The article compares two independent refiners: Delek US and Par Pacific. Par Pacific operates four facilities across the Pacific Northwest and Hawaii with integrated retail operations, demonstrating stronger profitability (4.9% net margin), lower leverage (0.8x debt-to-equity), and superior cash generation ($296.5M free cash flow). The author recommends Par Pacific as the better 2026 investment due to its niche market positioning, operational momentum, and cleaner financial profile.
<<<INSIGHTS>>>
DK | negative | High leverage (11.7x debt-to-equity), declining revenue (-9.5% YoY), net loss of $22.8M, low current ratio (0.8x), minimal free cash flow ($22M), concentrated customer base (12% of revenue), and regulatory uncertainty around renewable fuel obligations make it a riskier investment.
